## Current checkpoint — October 18

### Reminder context and device actions retain their original account — October 18

Application82cf017 follows the component/helper storage audit into private reminder trip/topic/progress context. Account-keyed enabled flags already existed, but pushApi fetched whatever SDK session was current without binding it to the originating context. It now requires original actor/current predicate/signal, validates the SDK user before fetch and checks current ownership before/after credentials, transport and JSON parsing. Authorization, no-store and owned signal are pinned. Existing endpoint bodies/server authorization remain. PushReminderControl internally keys by actual account, owns settings/action context, uses one synchronous action flight and rejects stale permission/service-worker/subscription/API completions before subsequent steps or local updates/toasts. Hour choices validate7–21 and update only after current success. Background sync cancels debounce and requests on account/context departure. No server routes/schema/scheduler were changed.

All1098tests/type-check pass; lint0errors/45existing warnings; audit0vulnerabilities; artifact19.80MiB passes guards; private-bank96markers/1332source/148static/0hits. Eight actual-component/production-lifecycle/API groups cover credential mismatch, abort/late parsing, pinned signal/auth/cache, duplicate permission, late device subscription, denied-permission retry, current exact subscribe body, departed unsubscribe, account/context settings replacement, cancelled background sync and validated/serialized/retryable hour changes. Priorf42fc78 helper sends synthetic A context using synthetic B credentials once where zero fetches are expected; current rejects mismatch. Fixture auth/session identities were stabilized and button text matching corrected before valid assertions. All permission/subscription/context/local flags and transport are synthetic: no actual browser permission, push binding/delivery, reminder preference or schedule was changed. Cancellation cannot undo accepted OS permission/subscription/server writes; departed subscriptions are not automatically unsubscribed because an endpoint can be shared with the current account. Hosted checks are combined-artifact regression checks; no hosted push request was performed. This is not a demonstrated live account compromise or hosted push bypass.

### Handwritten exam callbacks belong to their mounted learner/item — October 18

Applicationa54d4fd includes45d4df1. HandwrittenExamAnswer now keys its inner view by actual account, attempt and item, with no guest ink view. Pad/change/clear/transcript/confirmation callbacks check current mounted ownership at invocation. Animation frames belong to the same lifecycle and cancel on departure, replacement or clear; cancelled/superseded callbacks cannot export or clear newer work. Confirmation uses the current transcript and current drawing PNG, directly validates nonempty/bounds and refuses duplicate confirmation. Manual review answer fields and device-only ink remain unchanged. Drawing stays pending through pre-export transcript edits. Original saved ink and quota memory survive departure for their own account/item; cleanup does not delete them.

An effect cleanup/setup replay test caught the need to capture ownership at event invocation rather than render; fixed before final checks. ### Draft reads and save acknowledgements preserve their mounted owner and uncertain retry — October 18

Application61a16b8 hardens useExamDraft around its existing one-mounted-attempt contract (internally keyed ExamRunner remains the boundary). Edit/index/sync/flush/clear/reload handlers check the mounted actual owner before work. Unknown frozen item IDs and submitted-attempt edits are refused. A save’s success/error checks its active owner, abort signal and matching controller before acknowledgement, storage/status changes or follow-up conflict work. A late accepted save after departure leaves the exact original persisted pending flight intact; reopening replays that request identity to learn its authoritative outcome. Old finalizers cannot clear a different controller. Existing pending bytes are not erased on unmount.

Server-copy reads for409conflicts, explicit choices and post-upload reload now own linked controllers and a newest-read version; unmount aborts all reads. Before synchronous adoption they validate actual owner/mount/signal, attempt/enrollment and frozen form ID/version/rubric/learner identity. Superseded conflict reads cannot reset a newer copy. Conflict choices reject input changed while checking. Post-upload reload coalesces, checks owner after flush before fetching, and refuses overwrite if pending input, sequence or revision changed during the read. Existing quota memory, uncertain save/route identities, bounded continuation, core/selected/flat behavior, explicit conflicts and original-owner recovery remain. Client cancellation cannot undo server acceptance; exact replay is how an uncertain save is recovered. This does not prove arbitrary standalone hook prop reuse or live session-revocation behavior.

All1083tests/type-check pass; lint0errors/45existing warnings; audit0vulnerabilities; artifact19.80MiB passes guards; private-bank96markers/1326source/149static/0hits. Seven added actual-hook groups cover late same-owner save after unmount plus byte-identical pending retention/exact replay, actor-lost/departed edit/index/sync/flush/clear/reload,409read cancellation, coalesced external reload/unmount, no read after departed flush, newest read versus older response, fresh local edits during read, and wrong attempt/enrollment/frozen form rejection. Prior6a7bd71 returns true for a late save where false is expected; current keeps the original pending bytes and later replays the exact request successfully. The baseline required explicit repository tsconfig before the valid comparison; the existing conflict test now asserts linked cancellation rather than identical signal-object identity. New hook dependency/ref-cleanup warnings were fixed before final suite/type/lint checks. All save replies, accepted-write uncertainty, account changes, storage and server copies are controlled fixtures; no actual hosted save/refresh/conflict, learner response/result, browser/SDK-expiry or physical acceptance was manufactured.

### Daily support actions own displayed plan/lesson context; HTTP denial does not restore guidance — October 18

Application1ab54ff adds production useDailyPlanActions to DailyLessonPlan’s practice acknowledgement and support-checkpoint start. One mounted lease/controller/ref lock covers both actions and their success/error/finally work. Context includes actual account, module/pair/selected plan, lesson/way, embedded attempt and reconnect epoch, plus displayed snapshot identity. Identical action/ID/context retries retain request identity. Current successful starts update the local checkpoint→attempt map and embed that attempt; later local resume sends no duplicate start. Existing-attempt and embedded-return handlers also require the current view and no pending action. A changed snapshot cannot inherit old busy/error state. Parent account/module/pair/plan remounts are preserved; lesson/way changes now invalidate action completion without requiring a remount.

Guidance refresh now labels current-plan checking and disables old support actions while loading. Failed HTTP reads clear displayed guidance; HTTP failure is never treated as a network-only cache recovery even if navigator.onLine says false. Actual network failures still restore the original owner-scoped saved plan with its existing explicit saved-copy notice. Generic load errors no longer claim that every failure is a connection problem. This clears displayed records, not original cached bytes, and is not offline role-revocation/deletion proof. Accepted server acknowledgements/starts cannot be undone by client cancellation; a navigation already handed to the browser cannot be retracted.

All1076tests/type-check pass; lint0errors/45warnings observed; audit0vulnerabilities; artifact19.79MiB passes guards; private-bank96markers/1324source/149static/0hits. Prior462a0a9 sends4requests where2are expected under duplicate controls; a second controlled comparison performs1late practice navigation after lesson change where0is expected. Current passes both boundaries. No actual support acknowledgement, checkpoint start, practice visit/navigation or current browser acceptance was performed.

### Runner submit/continue/conflict completion owns the current attempt — October 18

Application7a12aa5 adds useExamRunnerActions around the existing draft/media engine. Actual account/attempt/form/learner/source/routing keys reset the runner’s confirmation state; guest views show no runner. Submit, continuation and conflict choice share a mounted lease, controller and synchronous ref lock. Submit requires explicit confirmation and a current active learner, checks recording/ink inputs, awaits the existing flush, checks ownership again before transport, rechecks inputs and sends exact attempt/revision with an owned signal. Same-context/revision retries retain request identity. Departed/blocked/core/unconfirmed/submitted/nonlearner callbacks cannot submit. Only a current successful request clears the submitted draft and invokes navigation. Conflict controls disable while working; stale Back/check-server/confirmation callbacks are guarded.

useExamDraft conflict reads now optionally receive the runner signal and check it before transport/adoption. Continuation links that signal to its existing controller/deadline, releases its abort listener and checks current ownership before caching the selected snapshot. Existing uncertain route/save identities, authoritative branching, device recovery, flat/selected/core controls and conflict contracts remain. Session-change checks precede abort classification to preserve existing recovery tests; a live deadline still raises AbortError. Cancellation cannot undo server acceptance, retract an already-invoked parent callback or cancel a save already running inside flush; the outer current check prevents the subsequent final submit after departure. Remaining draft helper callbacks are not declared fully audited.

All1070tests/type-check pass; lint0errors/45warnings observed; audit0vulnerabilities; artifact19.79MiB passes guards; private-bank96markers/1320source/149static/0hits. Six production-action-hook groups cover shared one flight, post-flush ownership, final request/signal/revision/retry identity, current-only clear/navigation, unmount/actor/context loss, fresh input checks, failed flush, capability/confirmation gates, continuation/conflict, explicit cancellation and old finalizer versus new work. Two actual runner source-boundary groups cover confirmation/reset/retained navigation and delayed flush/unmount. Two additional real draft-hook groups prove conflict signal forwarding/late-copy refusal and linked continuation cancellation retaining uncertain route identity. Existing ten routing-hook and two runner rendering groups also pass. Prior786b2f4 runner sends1request where the single-click delayed-flush/unmount assertion expects0; current source passes including duplicate refusal. Initial TS boundary extraction, missing synthetic readSpeechTake and cancellation-versus-session error ordering were corrected before final validation. All saves/submissions/continuations/recording flags/callbacks in these new trials are synthetic; no hosted submission, branch selection, conflict choice, consent, speech, scoring result or browser/SDK-expiry acceptance was manufactured.

### Review queue pagination and claim-to-open work own the current actor/team/view — October 18

Applicationd77102e internally keys ExamReviewQueue state/disclosure by actual actor and organization. Initial reads own mounted leases and AbortSignals. Refresh invalidates the old view, hides prior rows and preserves the current disclosure. Pagination and claim/open share a synchronous ref lock and owned controller; duplicate clicks cannot start another request before rerender. Rows deduplicate by attempt ID, with updated server rows winning. Current page identity rejects retained old-row/cursor handlers. Late page/claim/error/navigation/finally callbacks cannot affect a replacement account/team/view or an unmounted queue. Current failures remain retryable;401/403 removes private rows/pagination. Existing personal-sharing disclosures, exact claim body/cursor/team scope, server eligibility/authorization and review history remain. Accepted claims cannot be undone or released/reassigned by client cancellation, and parent navigation already invoked cannot be retracted.

### Rubric score and claim-to-resolution requests own reviewer form/evidence context — October 17

Application2060e08 includes1a02a0f. ExamRubricReview internally keys state by actual actor, attempt, review permission, frozen rubric version and protected attachment identity/availability. Guest/lost-access views do not show the review form. Response selection resets decision/points/reason/readiness; guarded controls disable while pending, and old form/media callbacks cannot affect replacement context. Score requests keep one flight and exact-body retry IDs. Direct checks require current review eligibility, a3–2000character reason, valid decision, finite points within displayed frozen bounds and current protected-speaking availability/readiness. Claim/resolve now share the owned mutation’s signal/current guard and lock: a late claim cannot send resolve after departure, and a late resolve cannot close another view. Already-owned reviews resolve directly; foreign ownership is refused. The shared mutation callback now receives its live signal/current predicate and exposes a synchronous pending check; prior callers remain compatible. Server authorization, qualification, calibration, rubric scoring and history remain unchanged. Client cancellation cannot roll back accepted writes or release/reassign claims; an accepted claim can remain assigned after departure.

### Word Match and Listening Drill generation guards — October 17

Source `158fad4` is locally verified and not deployed. Both active generators now reject missing/foreign Origin before any cache access, emit no-store and use the existing durable content allowance before paid misses. ## Release coordination

Claude desktop Code session “Languagethreshold.com model upgrades” owns the lesson release in `/Users/drive/lt-worktrees/app-haiku-5-5`. Codex owns dashboard and Exams work in `/Users/drive/lt-worktrees/admin-exams`, branch `codex/admin-exams`, initially based on a739580. No shared working-file edits.

The user authorized sending this review and the implementation plan to Claude. The handoff was sent through that session and acknowledged. Claude agreed to resolve the five lesson findings and leave admin/Exams work to Codex.

Canonical specification: https://chatgpt.com/space/page_8c02d8da6fa88191a18d9a2f4b75a5c5 (v1.2).

## Lesson preview findings

Tested the active localhost:3210 preview, desktop and 390×844 phone. These findings must be retested after the final deployment.

Verified: six learning modes, previous/next lesson controls, typing feedback for an accent error, full Reader return to the same lesson, speaking mission opens the correct lesson and Back to Lesson returns to the same Say step. Phone next-step button is reachable by scrolling. Anonymous speaking correctly requires sign-in.

Five findings sent to Claude:

1. See it described contextual reading but displayed a 32-item materials glossary. Thirty Roofing lessons shared five reading seeds. Claude accepted and is adding thirty lesson-specific passages with progression.
2. Handwriting recognition took roughly thirty seconds and fell back to self-comparison. Try again erased the drawing. Claude accepted preserving strokes, separate Check again/Start over and a timeout. Recognition success is not verified by this run.
3. React logged an update to WriteMode during HandwritingPad rendering. Claude accepted the callback fix.
4. The outer six learning modes and inner six practice modes used different lists and completion counters. Claude accepted removing duplicate kit navigation inside a lesson.
5. Desktop automatic scrolling hid heading/step controls behind the fixed header. Claude accepted a scroll offset fix.

## Dashboard baseline and repairs

Production review found progress below large billing/invitation forms, a horizontally scrolling phone roster, an assignment form without Cancel, and a requirement checkbox that did not start from the current assignment. These are usability issues independent of the certificate fixture.

An earlier certificate demonstration used fabricated completion data. It is not evidence of real learner activity or a synchronization defect. Demonstration records must stay clearly labeled and separate; do not issue real credentials from QA data.

Draft repairs: progress before setup in document order; two-column phone summary; learner cards on phones; collapsed billing/invitations; assignment edit defaults preserved when switching away and back; Cancel; distinct labels for recorded practice, self-reported mastery and certificate test; zero-score display; General language label for legacy core identifier; assigned-module-only Core 30 summaries to avoid sharing unrelated personal study.

UI verified with a clearly labeled local QA fixture and no real writes/email. Tests prove defaults and Cancel; fixture showed 0/25 correctly. Production sign-in, backend integration, real learner→admin synchronization and final deployment remain outstanding.

## Source catalog baseline

See `module-readiness-baseline-2026-10-15.json`. Source inventory before Claude’s release:

- 59 modules, 1,510 lessons.
- 16 travel modules have 15 lessons; Sport Fishing has 10. These need expansion to meet the requested 30-lesson standard.
- Only Roofing has authored `words` on every lesson (30/30). Generated deck availability must be audited after Claude’s release.
- No lesson has an explicit level field in the existing curriculum schema.
- Structural presence of a tool or generated list is not proof of reviewed content, objective alignment, offline readiness or mastery measurement.

## Build sequence

1. Finish and verify the lesson release on production, including the five fixes, desktop/phone flows, lesson-specific reading, signed-in speaking return and honest offline behavior.
2. Finish dashboard repairs, integrate the lesson release into the isolated branch, verify actual backend data boundaries and real learner tracking without changing Toby’s mastery or sending invitations.
3. Build one durable Exams engine with explicit personal/team enrollment scope, server authentication/ownership, frozen versioned forms and rubric, answer autosave, idempotent submission and retry. Preserve the old team certificate quiz separately.
4. Ship Roofing entrance, day 45 and day 90 forms first. Six skill dimensions; deterministic fixed items plus production rubrics; uncertain recognition is pending/not assessed, never a wrong answer. Copying is practice, not recall evidence. Entrance and exit use equivalent full-module coverage; midpoint comparisons use matched objectives. Calendar offsets are +44/+89 days from day one.
5. Permanent Exams area for individuals and teams: start/resume, schedule, results/history/comparison and targeted practice. Personal results stay private and are never inherited by joining a team.
6. Admin Exams overview, learner skill timeline, review queue and evidence-linked support module recommendations. Admin approves team additions; independent learner approves personal additions. Show workload before assigning.
7. Audit every module/language against the Roofing standard: 30 complete lessons, level/objective/prerequisite-driven toolkit, six learning modes, contextual usage, reviewed vocabulary/passages/assets and all three exams. Release in tracked batches, with draft/in-review/ready states rather than claims of completion from generation alone.
8. Versioned shared packs, local practice/save recovery and explicit network requirements support offline development. Validate browser→API→database→result→authorized dashboard end to end, plus privacy, scoring, scheduling and idempotency tests.

## Six-mode visit tracking correction in progress

The lesson preview still maps See/Recall/Hear/Type/Say to legacy step indices and maps Write to -1; the lesson list consequently reports 3/3 opened for a six-mode lesson. The Codex branch now has a separate `wayVisits` contract and reducer action, preserving original step records and first-visit times across cloud merges. Opening handwriting alone counts toward lessons opened without implying mastery. Three new migration/merge/state tests pass; 41 relevant tests and TypeScript pass; lint has zero errors and existing app-state Fast Refresh warnings. Wiring the new runner and its UI counts is pending integration of Claude’s release.

An isolated local PostgreSQL 16.14 database, `lt_exams_qa_20261015_codex`, was created for later migration and transactional behavior tests. No assessment schema has been applied to production. The browser’s current account has one active owner membership and no learner membership; a dedicated test learner has been requested for real multi-account reporting verification. No existing member roles or business records were changed.

## Shared lesson definition prepared

Added `lesson-definition.ts` and explicit Roofing teaching settings. All thirty Roofing objectives have stable IDs, earlier-lesson prerequisite links, source content/effort, a language-track identity, version reference and six-mode contract. Settings progress from supported words/phrases to connected responses and independent scenarios, with lesson-specific response contexts. Translation and replay suggestions are supports, not accessibility locks or proficiency ratings. Another language track cannot silently inherit Spanish content. Three relevant definition tests and TypeScript pass.

These definitions intentionally remain draft. Runtime wiring, contextual listening/response activity implementation, asset manifests and curriculum/release review remain required; metadata alone does not complete those requirements.

## Speaking stays in the lesson; download protocol hardened — October 16

Source `0391ce4` is READY in true Preview `dpl_CirJk9aAFdPXsHwGYtno1AZaF8vz`, https://language-threshold-om7sbvsh6-adobetoby-5572s-projects.vercel.app. Direct review link: `/?module=roofing&tab=core30&target=Spanish&native=English&lesson=1&way=say`.

Say it now opens the existing speaking mission component inside the lesson instead of dispatching to the separate Speak tab. The exact lesson prefix selects the mission from the current language-pair catalog; missing missions produce an explicit local-practice fallback, never the unrelated full catalog. Embedded setup and recap have Close practice; legacy request/return markers are not consumed or acted on by the embedded instance. The shared standalone Speak area retains its normal catalog and free-conversation controls. Existing sign-in, server age-attestation/consent checks, account gating, advisory-feedback boundaries and mission processing are reused. Embedded practice activates the existing speaking privacy mode. Leaving the step unmounts the mission and uses its existing request/audio/recognition cleanup; a real microphone session was not started by QA.

Local rehearsal is available without a microphone or connection. The task and word bank come from the current lesson. Roofing's supported lessons show the bank initially; independent lessons offer it on request. Online AI opens only on the learner's button press; going offline unmounts it and leaves a clear connection notice and local rehearsal task. Closing or changing steps ends that practice session, explicitly explained in the UI. These changes do not create an official speaking score or mark mastery.

Downloads now negotiate protocol 1 before sending install/status/remove commands. An older worker that does not respond fails the six-second handshake before receiving an install command, avoiding an unsupported four-minute install wait. A controller replacement during a check fails with a reload/recheck message. Initial status checks rerun on controller change and ignore stale results. Protocol acknowledgement itself reads no cache or remote content. Unit tests simulate the old worker, successful negotiation and controller replacement; those are not claims of an observed production upgrade.

Actual exact-Preview guest trials: lesson1 → Say it → embedded mission1 with matching words and A2 label; preserved sign-in modal; Close practice stays in the same Say step; next lesson opens mission2 (Deck, Underlayment & Edges); previous returns to lesson1; standalone Speak still shows its full scenario catalog/free-conversation tabs. No authentication, consent acceptance, microphone access, AI conversation, mastery or exam submission was performed in these trials. Existing standalone catalog copy says Roofing works in “Spanish or Spanish”; this is a minor inherited copy issue to repair separately.

The updated pack downloaded successfully through the negotiated worker. Its version is `8fa281145f7dc83770f418d23a48ea89efd4502167ac1d3bced71510af3dbd4e`, with 131 app assets and 10,790,640 bytes. Chrome DevTools Network was set to Offline with Disable cache OFF. Normal reload/hydration succeeded; lesson1 Say showed its local task/visible bank and a disabled AI button with the offline notice. Previously unvisited lesson30 opened offline and showed the independent crew-brief/change/handoff task with an optional bank. No claimed audio/dictation success or scored outcome. Networking was restored to No throttling, DevTools closed, viewport reset and the local test tab closed. The exact Preview lesson tab remains open for review.

## Reader account cache and retry checkpoint — October 16

Source `6b4e2ae` includes Reader engine `3eb1b49`; true Preview `dpl_6s4NXcwDXQvgVWE4XbQ47Rze66Pb`:
https://language-threshold-5u53sd1cx-adobetoby-5572s-projects.vercel.app

Reader previously imported global `lt.library.custom.v1` into whichever user signed in, removed that save after upload, and did not persist signed-in texts after a failed insert despite saying they were saved locally. It now uses an account/guest-owned device cache, stable UUIDs, a durable insert/delete queue and explicit sync status/Retry. A new text must reach device storage before the modal closes or a cloud request starts. Retried inserts ignore an existing UUID and confirm the owned row, preserving a newer server copy. Deleting during an uncertain upload keeps a durable deletion record. Reconnect retries; batches and requests are bounded. Identity changes/cancellation reject stale responses. Owner envelopes also separate reading preferences. Seed and generated public reading still use the existing reducer; private books use the device replica. Browser storage failure refuses an unsaved new text; cloud reading remains possible without pretending an offline copy was saved.

The old global library is left untouched and is not automatically imported. A notice explains unknown ownership. **Explicit legacy recovery remains unfinished.** No schema, grant, policy or role changes. RLS is still authoritative; caches never authorize cloud access.

Hosted SDK test used existing dedicated QA accounts: a real insert followed by an explicitly injected lost acknowledgement kept the pending queue; reload/retry produced exactly one owned row. Another learner could not read/delete that row, and RLS rejected an insertion with a mismatched owner even when a negative-test client actor check was deliberately wrong. Stale identity was rejected before a query. Those API fixture rows were removed. Evidence: `qa-reader-library-api-2026-10-16.json`.

Browser on preceding `3eb1b49` Preview: a labeled guest passage was translated and saved through the actual Add Text modal; sign-in did not import it. A different labeled personal passage saved through the modal reached exactly one personal cloud row and zero rows visible to the other learner. Guest imports remained zero for both accounts. This used actual unscored practice; its ordinary XP is not a proficiency score.

The offline test exposed a routing regression in the preceding lesson-address patch: cached Core 30 could overwrite an incoming Reader URL while profile loading was pending. `lessonOverviewLocation` now changes only the matching Core 30 URL, and old return markers cannot override an explicit different tab/module/pair. Four regression cases were added. The final Preview passed online Reader entry from cached Core 30, and an offline restart with Core 30 cached preserved the Reader address and reached the Reader.

Exact final Preview: the QA individual signed in normally, downloaded the current complete pack, loaded the cloud text into its own cache, then used Chrome Network Offline with Disable cache OFF. Reload restored a valid signed-in app; the cached personal passage remained in My Texts. The actual visible native Read button opened its English and Spanish paragraphs with DevTools still open and the throttling control explicitly still Offline. The final screenshot was captured in that state. Earlier intermediate attempts with DevTools closed were not counted as final offline-rendering proof. Afterward No throttling was explicitly restored and DevTools closed. Sign-out settled to guest with zero private text nodes; the second QA learner signed in (identity checked without printing it) and saw neither private nor guest QA text. Real owner accounts were untouched. One browser-created personal QA book is intentionally retained for subsequent acceptance; a guest-only QA book remains on the preceding Preview origin. API fixtures are distinct and cleaned.

487 tests, typecheck, lint zero errors/nine warnings (the six prior warnings plus three existing helper-export warnings in the newly linted library file), production audit zero vulnerabilities, build/artifact 19.35 MiB passed. Exact final Preview thirteen Exams API groups passed. Private-bank audit: 96 markers, 864 source/139 static files, zero hits. Current pack: `d856c49986cbcbac0ae8cfe0285978b870d8183151725457104e2c55d5d63772`, 131 assets/10,677,282 bytes.

Proof: `qa-reader-library-preview-2026-10-16.json`; screenshot `/Users/drive/git-backups/language-threshold-reader-owned-offline-2026-10-16.png`. Browser sizing was not changed this turn; DevTools temporarily narrowed the page. Final tab is Roofing lesson1/See on the new Preview using the dedicated team QA learner. Networking is normal.

Other private device stores (annotations, flashcards, Tutor/Speak history) remain to audit/isolate. ## Failed sign-out/profile restoration overlap repaired — October 16

The overlap above is fixed in source. Auth now keeps `isCurrentUser` tied to the actual identity and exposes a separate stable `canUseReader` guard for the Reader replica and remote adapter. Pending sign-out still freezes Reader writes/sync; profile restoration can finish and release incoming lesson-link initialization while the session remains valid. Cancellation or SDK failure restores Reader access. Successful sign-out still changes identity and invalidates the saved Reader epoch as before.

Three regression cases exercise the production access guard with the real deferred-profile coordinator, sign-out coordinator and Reader device engine: successful profile response during failed sign-out, failed profile response during failed sign-out, and cancelled sign-out with Reader edits resuming. They check navigation readiness, avoid enabling profile writes after a failed profile read, reject Reader edits during the warning and reject the previous identity after an account switch.

Local verification: **504 tests pass**, type-check clean, lint zero errors/nine existing warnings, production build and artifact verification pass (19.36 MiB server bundle). The built artifact/private-source audit checks96 markers across874 source candidates/143 static text assets with zero hits. This change has not yet been deployed or browser-tested; the exact Preview at the top remains application `e0c9e18`. Live remains `f9f8685`. No database, speech-policy, reviewer-role or schedule changes were made.

Next available engineering work is the remaining globally keyed private stores: `lt.annotations.v1`, `lingualens.flashcards.v2`, `lt.tutor.v1` and `lt.speak.v1`. Preserve unowned legacy data for explicit recovery rather than assigning it to the next signed-in learner. Then stage the combined fixes and verify account switching, Reader recovery and lesson entry on the resulting exact deployment. All prior human content, calibration, pilot, owner-retention and companion-domain release gates remain open.

## Account-owned notes/cards/conversation persistence — October 16

The Notes, Flashcards, Tutor and Speak providers now use a shared account/guest device-store adapter. Each record includes a schema, data kind and owner, all checked when reading. Storage keys distinguish user IDs and guest explicitly. The adapter rejects stale signed-in callbacks after identity changes; the hook also stops writes after its account-keyed subtree unmounts. Existing per-language thread/card/practice shapes remain inside each owner's record. Own saved work can be restored on reauthentication; it is not offered to a different learner or the signed-out guest.

## Public game cache context repair — October 16

All five game generators now use a bounded shared public-context cache encoding language, level, effective CEFR, grammar axis where relevant, topic and the exact avoid array as structured JSON. Conjugation, Listening, Idioms and False Friends previously omitted topic; Word Match concatenated topic and avoid text ambiguously. Requests with personal userWords bypass BOTH cache reads and writes, without putting those words into shared keys. This repairs the observed source risk; no real cross-account disclosure was reproduced.

Conjugation now validates required strings, difficulty/axis, exactly one blank and four distinct normalized answer options. Duplicate or malformed choices are rejected with retry; the previous invented infinitive-question-mark padding is removed. These are structural checks, not a semantic grammar verdict: the observed Yo/revisé contradiction remains unresolved and blocks calling the game content ready.

Seven added tests bring the suite to567 passing, type-check clean, lint0 errors/40 warnings. Two tests execute the actual production Conjugation/Word Match handlers with network SDK fixtures, proving public hits, topic separation, personal bypass in both directions, and rejection without caching/padding malformed Conjugation output. Other tests cover all public key dimensions, concatenation collisions, Unicode/case duplicate options and bounded eviction. No new keys/models, schema, policies, official exam results, certificates, speech collection or schedules. Preview build/hosted verification of these edits is pending; the current b6 Preview remains source1277ed8.

## Phone account menu and Conjugation review gate in source — October 16

AuthButton now uses a nonmodal dropdown. On a phone the enclosing More sheet retains its existing modality/focus handling; the account dropdown no longer acquires a second body-pointer lock. The observed stale lock occurred after account-keyed providers unmounted the sheet and menu together. This targets that nested teardown cause without globally clearing styles or weakening authentication. Browser repeat of sign-out→guest interaction→sign-in is required before calling the defect fixed.

Each newly generated structurally valid Conjugation item now receives a separate Haiku5.5 consistency request. The checker gets the sentence, options in sorted order, explanation and requested difficulty/axis; it does not get the designated correct-answer field. It identifies all fitting options and must find exactly one matching the author's answer, with unambiguous context, consistent explanation/axis and appropriate difficulty. Missing/malformed/negative/ambiguous verdicts return a retry message and are never cached. This adds one bounded450-output-token model request per uncached candidate, with no model/key changes. It is an AI consistency gate, not a native-speaker approval or calibrated exam grading. Live-model sampling and learner retry usability remain required.

The existing production-handler test now exercises empty/wrong/multiple fitting answers, contradictory explanation, ambiguity, axis and difficulty rejection; rejected candidates do not seed the cache, and a later valid candidate remains uncached until accepted. It also verifies the checker input omits the designated answer.567 tests pass; lint0 errors/40 warnings. Type-check and the new Preview/browser trials are pending. Full release/content/privacy/retention/pilot/59-module parity gates remain open.

## Durable lesson/deck generation limits and final-response headers — October 16

The preceding turn was progress: explicit saved culture readings and loaded-Reader offline reuse were implemented and verified. The guard requires exact Origin and the canonical platform IP, HMAC-hashes its namespaced counter key and uses the existing server-only atomic RPC. Caller-selectable forwarding fallbacks are refused. Missing platform identity or failed/timeout budget storage returns503 without a model call; exceeded minute/hour windows return429 with useful retry copy. Field-deck errors now preserve that copy. ## October16 — conversation entry budgets, bounded history and real Tutor acceptance

The preceding turn made progress by bounding paid handwriting checks in an independent pool, fixing shared-output test harness collisions and retaining the live expiry workflow. This turn protects the4legacy conversation routes: Tutor, missionary discussion and both field-prep variants. They now validate strict Origin before reading input, read at most96KiB of UTF-8 JSON (including streamed bodies without a trustworthy length header), parse the existing schema and then claim a durable conversation budget before any model request. Invalid/origin/oversize bodies spend no model budget. Conversation defaults are30/IP/minute,300/IP/hour and600/environment/hour, separate from content and handwriting pools. Request defaults still require real team throughput/cost validation; no dollar spending guarantee is claimed. Success/error/companion responses now say no-store. Scoring/authored exam contracts are unchanged.

The4clients previously sent whole histories, including empty interrupted replies; sufficiently long conversations could exceed the existing40/80message schemas. They now send at most20recent nonempty whole turns/40KiB of message content, starting at a real user turn when one remains. The latest question stays whole; oversized individual input fails explicitly. Full visible/stored local history is not pruned. Scenario opening still sends an empty history; failed/aborted empty replies no longer poison the next request. Existing Reader context and level/module payloads remain separate and intact. React review checked use within existing try/catch, no source history mutation, no new render-time effects or provider identity changes.

## October16 — rejected oversized turn cannot poison later retries

A follow-up checks the remaining conversation retry edge: a user can paste a turn above a route's individual character limit, then send a shorter valid question. Keeping the rejected turn in outbound history would make every later request fail schema validation. Recent-history selection now refuses an oversized latest turn explicitly, keeps its original local text, and omits earlier rejected oversized turns from later requests. Missionary discussion uses its actual3000character limit; Tutor/field-prep use8000. A production-helper regression covers both limits and the successful shorter retry. No visible/local history is deleted or silently truncated.

## Handwriting survives responsive layout changes — October 16

The actual TEJA response above exposed a separate usability defect: resizing the602×206 desktop pad to288×174 phone size left its original pixel coordinates unchanged and clipped the A. Application077b930 keeps a stable writing coordinate space and uniformly fits it inside the visible pad. Pointer positions and pressure widths map back into that space. Original strokes remain unchanged, avoiding cumulative shrinkage across rotations and preserving the recognition export. New ink in letterbox margins is included in the next fit; Clear starts a fresh space at the current size. Resizing ends the active stroke to avoid joining unrelated positions. Guide and ink share the transform; Undo/Clear and manual review remain available. There is no extra model request, assessment score or mastery action.

Four geometry regressions cover final-letter containment and proportions;100phone/desktop cycles without coordinate/width mutation; pointer/pen-width alignment after resizing; and new margin ink on the next resize. ## Targeted supporting checks use their assigned forms and units — October 16

The PRD13 targeted-unit path exposed a server mismatch: listEnrollments tested only purpose=module forms, while Start selected the capability's before/after support forms. A targeted enrollment could therefore inherit a full-module Ready flag, or fail to advertise its actual reviewed short check. Applicationc4d62c7 adds one server-only supporting-check resolver shared by the overview and Start. Ordinary full-module supporting enrollments retain their own three module exams; targeted units never acquire a Day45 check from a module form.

After the existing actor/team enrollment authorization, the resolver reads only capability IDs and child assignments from that authorized page, then only the pinned before/after form IDs. Limits are50enrollments, at most100pinned forms and100assignment rows; overflow/errors fail503 instead of returning a partial readiness claim. It requires a verified unit mapping in the same release, exactly one active/checkpoint-due unit assignment for the same learner/team, and matching frozen capability/version/release/module/language pair. Assigned unit IDs must be distinct, belong to the reviewed mapping and exist in this module's current curriculum. Each selected unit needs a corresponding item in that exact pinned support form. No full-module/other-capability/release form can substitute. Official readiness still requires current complete reviewed lesson bytes and a verified calibrated form; QA availability stays separate. Existing saved attempts/history remain readable when new-start readiness fails.

The resolver returns form ID plus a reviewed boolean internally, never definition bytes. Only the existing ready/pilotReady booleans enter the overview. Private form definitions are read on the server solely to check selected-objective coverage; no question/answer fields enter a public pack, profile, new cache or overview response. Start applies the same mapping/coverage checks before its existing current-binding, listening and authoritative database start rules. No table schema, roles, grants, approvals, schedules, learner assignments or scoring rules changed. The current Supabase changelog and existing JavaScript filter documentation were consulted; no SDK/API migration was needed. Security review retained server-only service access, database-backed actor/team authorization and client-denied assessment tables.

Verification:789tests, clean type-check, lint0errors/41warnings, build/artifact19.64MiB. Eight new production-core/controller tests cover pinned targeted versus full-module forms, all selected-unit coverage, source-unit existence, stale/foreign/duplicate/paused mappings, lesson review, failed/bounded reads, actor denial, no private-key output and QA-versus-official readiness. The real POST handler is exercised with controlled auth/billing/budget/final-serialization boundaries; the resolver, form selection and lesson binding run actual production code. Those fixtures contain explicitly fictional review claims and never enter the hosted project. The complete named-local database integration suite separately passes support permissions/budget/lifecycle, targeted/full schedules, frozen evidence/retries and client denial, then ROLLBACK.
